The unlock was re-reading the function from scratch instead of reasoning forward from the document's characterisation of it, and the payoff was the whole format: a single no-search walk landing exactly on EOF in 100,384/100,384 shipped entries. **When a gap has survived several passes, re-derive the one sentence that defines it before spending effort inside it** — an inherited analogy ("this is like the one next to it") is exactly the kind of claim that gets written once and never re-checked. One level up from the "an offset without an explicit base is not a location" lesson: **a mechanism named by analogy is not a mechanism identified.**
- **A control that a downstream alignment absorbs is not a control (2026-09-13, `spec-physics-format.md` §4.4.6(e)).** That document had recorded a byte cost as "not measurable from shipped data" because 0/4/8/12/16/20 bytes all landed identically — true as measured, but an artifact of the instrument: a 16-byte align downstream swallows any 0–15-byte shift, and a *searched* anchor downstream swallows the rest. Once the search was removed, the same quantity became trivially measurable (it is a size field in the block) and substituting the assumed `0` dropped the landing rate from 100.0000% to 88.8508%. The same defect appeared in this pass's own first `±4`-byte control, which silently reported the baseline's score until widened to `±16`. **Before citing a null result from a perturbation, work out what absorbs the perturbation, and size it to exceed every slack term between it and the observable.**
- **`getBookmarks(Address, String)` in Ghidra's API takes a bookmark TYPE (`"Note"`), not a category** — querying it with a category name returns 0 for bookmarks that were written perfectly. Cost a false "0/9 bookmarks persisted" scare on 2026-09-13, caught only because the plate comments at the same nine addresses reported 9/9. Iterate `getBookmarksIterator("Note")` and filter on `getCategory()`. Another instance of the standing "a verification that reports failure can itself be the bug" rule, in the tooling layer.
- **Transferring a table between documents without its clarifying prose can silently change what the table means (2026-09-14, `spec-physics-format.md` §4.4.6(h)).** A material-record field-offset table was copied whole from `spec-foliage-format.md` §6 into the physics doc as "the same function, so the same layout" — true, but the source document's one load-bearing sentence right before its own table ("Each record is length-prefixed: `u32 size`, then 8-align, a `0x30` header...") never made the trip, leaving the copy's own `+0x00` looking like it meant "the record's own raw start" instead of "the aligned header, 4+ bytes in." Caught by Team B while implementing the table into their reader, not by this side. Verified independently before writing the fix (re-read the source doc's original prose and the actual decompile, not just the relay) — the underlying mechanism was never wrong; only the copy had silently dropped the one sentence that anchored what the offsets were relative to. **A table's offsets are only as portable as the sentence that states their origin — copy that sentence too, every time, not just the rows.**
- **Joint lesson with Team B, 2026-09-14 (`spec-physics-format.md` §4.4.6(i)) — recorded distinctly because it belongs to neither side alone.** A relayed count (113 failures) disagreed with this side's own first reproduction (~1,417) by over 10×. Neither side's first number was right, and neither side's own further static reasoning alone would have found the real cause: this side's own harness had a real alignment-gated search bug (missing non-16-aligned g-side continuation offsets), separately masking that Team B's own *production parser* had a real, independently-confirmed shipped bug (an unconditional header-displacement offset never wired to their own already-correct conditional-align8 rule, used properly elsewhere in their own codebase for a sibling format). Only a direct, mutual byte-level trace of one disputed file — both sides reading the identical raw bytes and comparing field-by-field, not further reasoning about either side's own code in isolation — surfaced both bugs and reconciled the count exactly (113 → 29 on their side, matching this side's own figure). **When two independent implementations disagree by a large factor and each side's own internal check keeps reporting itself as correct, stop iterating separately and trade raw bytes at the exact disputed position — a shared ground truth resolves what two isolated self-checks cannot.**

- **A negative claim about what a function reads or writes needs an exhaustive scan, not a comparison against another document's field list (found 2026-09-20).** Agent N reported that the customization assembler `FUN_009f36d0` "does not touch" the ID-13 stash fields `+0x1a8`/`+0x1ac` because the assembler's known field list (`spec-morph-format.md` §12.3) lacked them; agent S then found — and the orchestrator confirmed from raw bytes (displacement `0x1a8` at VA `0x9f3ee3`, `0x1ac` at `0x9f3ebd`/`0x9f3ef4`/`0x9f3f12`, and a call to the rig loader `0x4bc810` at `0x9f3f29`) — that it reads both. A checked list is only as complete as the last pass that built it. **How to apply:** for any "function F never references X" claim, byte-scan F's whole body for the displacement/immediate and cross-reference the callee set, and label it CONFIRMED only after that; otherwise label it "not found in the fields listed so far".

- **A spec's own "ignore this field / it is scratch" conclusion is a claim of absence and needs the same exhaustive-scan bar (found 2026-09-20).** `spec-vpp-container.md` recorded the exact loader accumulation that locates non-first mode-(a) entries, then discarded it as write-only because no reader of `+0x04` was found; every later pass read entries at the logical `+0x08` and concluded the problem was compression, through 3 static + 1 dynamic pass. Team B, reading the same spec, found the rule at once. **How to apply:** when a loader computes a value and no reader is found, TEST the value as the missing offset/size/flag before calling it scratch; when many passes fail the same way, re-read what the loader computes rather than adding decode variations (cf. the skinning-seam lesson: a search confined to one class of variation cannot find a defect of another class).

## 6. Status at Time of Original Writing (2026-09-07)

Both quick-fix corrections (§1a) and the Ghidra bookmarks/comments (§2a, the first 15) were complete and verified (Ghidra headless run confirmed all 15 addresses marked and the project save succeeded). That was the last piece of the peer's priority-shift request at the time. **Work continued afterward — see §7.**

---

## 7. Session Update (2026-09-08) — The Count×Stride Investigation, Full Account

The peer approved pursuing HANDOFF.md §4's old item 1 ("get a richer sample, re-test count×stride"), then explicitly approved a follow-on to fully close it out via raw disassembly. This section is the complete record of that work — both what it resolved and where it hit a real wall — since none of this exists anywhere else and the user asked for it to be written down before continuing further.

### 7.1 The richer sample

Scanned `characters.vpp_pc` and `customize_item.vpp_pc` for `.ccmesh_pc`/`.gcmesh_pc` entries by size (new tooling: `scan_meshes.py`, a generic container walker that collects mesh-entry paths+sizes without fully decoding them — much faster than extracting everything). Found real character body meshes at 300–400 KB (`.gcmesh_pc` side) — dramatically bigger than the original `cm_hand_f_leather` glove sample (70 KB). Picked `chester.ccmesh_pc` (6,488 bytes) / `chester.gcmesh_pc` (251,364 bytes) from `characters.vpp_pc/deckers_male_chester.str2_pc` — a full NPC character body mesh, ~4× the old sample's `.ccmesh_pc` size, with 9 real texture references (skin, hair, cloth) vs. the glove's 5. Extracted with new tooling `extract_named.py` (pulls specific named entries out of a container by full path, handling nested `.str2_pc`). Both files are saved in the scratchpad alongside the original samples.

### 7.2 What got resolved (all now in `spec-geometry-format.md` §4.1.2)

1. **The fixed sub-header is exactly `0x88` (136) bytes** — confirmed directly from `FUN_007527b0` (the real, previously-unexamined caller of `FUN_007524f0`), not inferred. Gives a complete formula: `array_region_start = (material-block-end, 16-byte aligned) + 0x88`.
2. **The "second, mixed-case name list" from `.ccmesh_pc`'s §3.2 "footer"** is confirmed via disassembly to be a real, length-prefixed structural field read immediately at the start of the array region (not a loose trailing appendage) — `FUN_007524f0` reads a 4-byte length and skips exactly that many bytes. Confirmed content on both samples: the mixed-case sibling of the material-block texture names.
3. **Array 4 (4-byte stride) is confirmed, byte-for-byte, to be a fixed 68-entry identity table (`0, 1, 2, ..., 67`)** — identical on both the glove and the character-body samples, despite the meshes being structurally unrelated. Very likely a fixed engine constant (a max-bone-influence or max-slot palette size), not mesh-specific data.
4. **The exact offset chain (sub-header+0x88, skip the name blob, walk arrays 1–4 by their real counts, 8-byte align) lands precisely on the nested "Mesh" sub-block's version field — and it reads exactly `9` on both independent real samples, from pure arithmetic with zero searching.** This is the strongest empirical validation available short of a live debugger (≈1/65,536 odds of a coincidental hit per sample; hit twice).
5. **The Mesh sub-block's header contains a field, immediately after its version field, that exactly equals the paired `.gcmesh_pc` file's own leading 4 bytes** — confirmed byte-for-byte on both samples (`chester`: `0x96115921` both places). This supersedes the earlier vague "somewhere in the ccmesh footer" cross-reference guess with an exact, disassembly-derived field location. (An earlier candidate location, the outer sub-header's own `+0x14` field, was checked and explicitly ruled out — it does *not* match, so don't re-guess it.)
6. **The count×stride hypothesis (`+0x20` count, `+0x30` stride) is now understood structurally: a flags-byte bit 0 in the Mesh sub-block selects between reading the resulting byte range inline from `.ccmesh_pc` (bit clear) or from the cross-referenced `.gcmesh_pc` buffer instead, via a separate function `FUN_00e71740` that reuses the same count/stride fields against a different base (bit set).** **On both real samples, bit 0 is set** — meaning the vertex data was never going to be found inline in `.ccmesh_pc`, which is almost certainly why the original test on the small sample read as inconclusive rather than simply wrong. The arithmetic itself: `16,888 × 2 = 33,776 bytes` (chester), `3,976 × 2 = 7,952 bytes` (glove) — both `u16`-element buffers.
7. **`FUN_00e71740` also performs a real cross-file integrity check**: it reads a `u32` at its own cursor position in the externally-referenced buffer and requires it to exactly equal `checkval1` (finding 5) — confirmed via disassembly.

### 7.3 Where it hit a real wall (the peer explicitly approved pushing this far; this is an honest stop, not a dead end)

Reading raw x86 instructions (not decompiler C output — the decompiler drops implicit register-passed arguments, which is exactly what's going on here) at each remaining call site revealed:

- `checkval1` is used by `FUN_00e71740` as an **expected value to verify against**, not as the base pointer for the external read. The actual base pointer is a *separate* value, forwarded untouched as a plain argument through `FUN_00e718b0` → `FUN_007524f0`'s own 3rd argument.
- Tracing that argument to its origin means walking back through `FUN_007524f0`'s caller (`FUN_007527b0`) to *its* caller — which is `FUN_00751f60`, the actual registered constructor for `.ccmesh_pc`/`.csmesh_pc` (confirmed via the registration table, appearing at two storage sites: `local_18 = FUN_00751f60;`).
- `FUN_00751f60` takes this value as its own 5th parameter — **meaning it is never computed by any `.ccmesh_pc`-specific code at all.** Checking every reference to `FUN_00751f60` in the binary found **only its two storage sites in the registration table — no direct `CALL FUN_00751f60` instruction exists anywhere.** The real invocation happens through an indirect call (a generic resource-construction dispatcher that reads a registered type's constructor pointer and calls through it uniformly for all ~45 registered types), which static cross-reference search cannot resolve back to a specific caller.

**This is the same category of boundary as the garage-hooks/PE-relocation-fixup scope-jump documented in `spec-vpp-container.md` §3.8** — a trace that legitimately runs into generic, shared engine infrastructure rather than format-specific code. Per this project's standing discipline, it's flagged and parked rather than chased further without explicit scoping. **Likely high value if picked up**: resolving the generic dispatcher would plausibly answer "how does any c-file locate its paired g-file" for every format in this project at once, not just `.ccmesh_pc` — but it's a distinct, bigger investigation (finding and reading a shared engine subsystem, not one function), and should be scoped and approved as its own target rather than assumed as a quick continuation.

### 7.4 New tooling this pass

- `scratchpad/scan_meshes.py` — generic container walker, collects `.ccmesh_pc`/`.gcmesh_pc` entry paths+sizes across an archive without fully decoding them. Reusable for finding "richer samples" of any resource type by size.
- `scratchpad/extract_named.py` — pulls specific named entries out of a container by exact full path (handles nested `.str2_pc`). Complements `extract_xtbl.py` (which only auto-saves `.xtbl` entries).
- `scratchpad/sim_ccmesh.py` — a from-scratch Python re-implementation of the confirmed `.ccmesh_pc` array-walk + Mesh-sub-block-header parse (§7.2 items 1–5), runnable against any real `.ccmesh_pc` file to print its material-block info, six-array layout, and Mesh sub-block fields. This is the fastest way to check a new sample without touching Ghidra at all.
- `tools/scripts/DecompileMeshChain.java`, `FindMeshCaller.java`, `DecompileHelpers.java`, `DisasmMeshCallers.java`, `FindOuterCaller.java`, `FindTopCaller.java` — one-off Ghidra headless scripts used to progressively decompile/disassemble `FUN_007524f0` → `FUN_007527b0` → `FUN_00e718b0`/`FUN_00e71410` → `FUN_00e71310`/`FUN_00e71740` → `FUN_00751f60` and their callers. Not needed again unless re-deriving this chain from scratch, but kept as a record of the exact method (each one's name describes what it did).
- `tools/scripts/AddMeshFollowupBookmarks.java` — added the 3 new bookmarks (`FUN_007527b0`, `FUN_00e71310`, `FUN_00e71740`) confirmed successful.
- `tools/scripts/AddDispatcherBoundaryBookmark.java` — adds a bookmark for `FUN_00751f60` documenting the scope-boundary finding. **Run successfully 2026-09-08 (confirmed: `marked 00751f60`, project save succeeded). `FUN_00751f60` is now bookmarked in Ghidra, same as every other address in §2a.**

### 7.5 Status: this section is now fully closed out

Both actions from the original "immediate next action" list are done: the `FUN_00751f60` bookmark is persisted, and the peer/user approved scoping a new, explicit investigation into the generic resource-construction dispatcher — see §8 below, which closed it out completely.

## 8. Session Update (2026-09-08, continued) — The Generic Resource-Dispatch Investigation, Full Account and Closure

The peer approved scoping the generic dispatcher as its own target, explicitly noting the payoff: if this really is how every c/g pair finds its partner file, solving it once would unlock the same answer for every other paired format, not just close out `.ccmesh_pc`'s last byte offset. **That's exactly what happened.** New document: `spec-resource-dispatch.md`.

### 8.1 Method

Started from `FUN_00daf4e0` (the registration table's consumer, already bookmarked) and found every other function in the binary that reads the global arrays it writes into. That surfaced a small, cohesive cluster of functions (`FUN_00dd22c0`, `FUN_00dd23f0`, `FUN_00dd29c0`, `FUN_00dd2a70`, `FUN_00dd35e0`, `FUN_00dd2e30`, plus a couple of unrelated ones like `FUN_006f7330` that turned out to be building cache-tagged paths, not part of the core mechanism). Decompiled all of them, then read raw x86 at the one call site that mattered (`FUN_00dd2e30`'s indirect constructor call) since the decompiler drops an argument there — the same pattern seen at every other call boundary in §7.

### 8.2 What got found (full detail in `spec-resource-dispatch.md`)

1. **A real correction to the registration row's field layout.** The original (pre-session) bookmark comment on `FUN_00daf4e0` labeled the row's two leading fields "size fields" — they're actually the **constructor and destructor function pointers**. The fields previously assumed to be part of the ctor/dtor group (at row offsets `+0x18`/`+0x20`) are actually the **two file-extension strings** (e.g. `.ccmesh_pc` and `.gcmesh_pc` as literal text). This was confirmed by tracing actual *usage* of each field across multiple functions, not just by re-reading the registration site once. **`spec-resource-dispatch.md` §2 has the full corrected table — treat it as authoritative over the old bookmark comment's field-purpose claims (the addresses themselves are still correct, only what several fields mean changed).**
2. **`FUN_00dd2a70` is the literal answer to "how does a c-file find its g-file."** Given a resource's base name and its registered type, it builds *both* filenames by combining the name with each of the type's two stored extension strings, resolves/opens both files, and stores both results into a shared descriptor object. **Pure filename convention — same base name, swap the extension — resolved generically for any type flagged as "paired," with zero content-based cross-referencing.** Two other functions (`FUN_00dd35e0`, `FUN_00dd29c0`) independently do the exact same thing, reinforcing the field identification.
3. **`FUN_00dd2e30` is the generic dispatcher itself** — the function that actually calls through a registered type's constructor pointer. It branches on the same "has a pair" flag: unpaired types get a 4-argument constructor call; paired types get a **6-argument** call, where the 6th argument is exactly the paired file's buffer from finding 2. Confirmed by reading the raw `PUSH`/`CALL` sequence at the indirect call site, since the decompiler only shows 4-5 arguments where 6 are actually pushed.
4. **This closes the `.ccmesh_pc` thread completely.** `.ccmesh_pc`'s constructor (`FUN_00751f60`, §7's scope boundary) has its 3rd parameter independently confirmed elsewhere to be the primary file's buffer — and that matches the dispatcher's 3rd pushed argument exactly. By the same positional logic, `FUN_00751f60`'s mystery 5th parameter (the one that flows all the way down to `FUN_00e71740`'s external-buffer base pointer in §7) is the dispatcher's 6th argument: **the `.gcmesh_pc` buffer, already resolved and loaded, handed over as a plain argument.** No content-based lookup, no hash, no runtime cross-referencing beyond the one already-documented 4-byte identity check (§7.2 finding 5).

### 8.3 What this means going forward

**This mechanism is generic — it will apply the same way to every other paired c/g format in this project** (`.clmesh_pc`/`.glmesh_pc`, `.csmesh_pc`/`.gsmesh_pc`, etc.), since it's gated purely on the registration table's per-type flag, not any per-format code. Concretely: whenever `.glmesh_pc`'s still-unresolved constructor stub (`LAB_0074c530`, HANDOFF §4 item 2) finally gets decompiled, its g-file buffer can be expected to arrive the same way — as a plain incoming argument — without needing to re-derive this whole mechanism again.

### 8.4 New Ghidra bookmarks this pass

`FUN_00dd2e30` (the dispatcher), `FUN_00dd2a70` (the paired-file resolver), `FUN_00dd22c0` (extension→type-ID resolver), `FUN_00dd35e0` (a second request-builder using the same pattern), and `FUN_00daf4e0` (comment corrected/superseded — see 8.2 finding 1) — all confirmed bookmarked and commented, Ghidra save succeeded. `FUN_00751f60`'s bookmark comment was also updated to state the resolved answer instead of the open question.

### 8.5 Status: fully closed, no loose ends

Everything from this thread — the original count×stride hypothesis, the exact cross-file reference mechanism, and the generic dispatch mechanism underneath it — is written up, cross-referenced between `spec-geometry-format.md` §4.1.2 and `spec-resource-dispatch.md`, and bookmarked in Ghidra. **The one remaining item (the literal byte offset) has since been finished too — see §9.**

## 9. Session Update (2026-09-08, final) — The Literal Byte Offset, Found and Verified

With the generic dispatcher fully understood (§8), the peer asked for the actual finish: trace `FUN_00e71310`'s cursor logic against the now-known `.gcmesh_pc` buffer to get the exact byte offset. This is the last piece of the entire multi-session `.ccmesh_pc`/`.gcmesh_pc` thread, and it closed cleanly.

**Finding:** `.ccmesh_pc`'s constructor (`FUN_00751f60`) initializes *two* cursors at entry, both to `0` — the main `.ccmesh_pc` cursor (already used throughout) and a **second cursor for `.gcmesh_pc`**, whose address gets threaded down as a hidden argument through 4 more function boundaries (`FUN_007527b0` → `FUN_007524f0` → `FUN_00e718b0` → `FUN_00e71740`), the decompiler dropping it at every single hop — traced via raw x86 disassembly at each call site, same method as everywhere else in this chain.

With that cursor confirmed to start at `0`: the first 4 bytes of `.gcmesh_pc` get consumed by the already-known cross-file integrity check (which is *why* `checkval1` matches `.gcmesh_pc`'s leading 4 bytes — they're the literal same field, read from both sides), the cursor aligns up from `4` to the next 16-byte boundary, and **the count×stride vertex/index data begins at exactly `.gcmesh_pc` file offset `0x10`** (for the common, single-buffer case — confirmed true on both real samples).

**Then verified independently against real file bytes** (not just disassembly): at offset `0x10`, `cm_hand_f_leather.gcmesh_pc` reads `0, 1, 2, 2, 3, 3, 3, 4, 5, 4, 6, 7, 8, 9, 10, ...` — smoothly ascending small integers, matching **this project's own original black-box observation of this exact region, made many sessions ago** before any of this mechanism was understood ("a long run of small, smoothly-increasing 16-bit values... roughly the first ~8,000 bytes" — the real declared region is 7,952 bytes, an almost exact match). `chester.gcmesh_pc` shows the same shape at a different numeric base.

**This closes the count×stride hypothesis three independent ways at once**: disassembly says the loader executes exactly this arithmetic at exactly this offset; real file bytes at that offset are structurally exactly what was predicted, on two unrelated samples; and it matches the very first black-box guess about this data, from before any of the intervening mechanism existed as a finding. Nothing further is needed on this specific question — it's as settled as anything in this project gets without literally single-stepping the game.

**Written up in:** `spec-geometry-format.md` §4.1.2 items 9–10 (full detail), §6 open item #9 (marked closed with strikethrough, per this project's convention for fully-resolved items). **New Ghidra bookmark:** `FUN_00e71740`'s comment updated with the complete, final answer.

**This is the end of the `.ccmesh_pc`/`.gcmesh_pc` count×stride thread** that ran from HANDOFF §4 item 1 (originally: "get a richer sample") through §7 (the richer sample and structural resolution) through §8 (the generic dispatcher) to here. No further action needed on it unless a genuinely new question comes up (e.g. the multi-stream alternate path, never seen on a real sample — noted as real follow-on detail, not a gap, in `spec-geometry-format.md` §4.1.2's closing paragraph).

---

## 10. Session Update (2026-09-08, continued again) — Two Independent Threads Resolved: an Implementation Bug Diagnosed, and `.glmesh_pc`'s Constructor Resolved

**Thread A — Team B's "98 richer meshes fail" report, diagnosed as their bug, not a format issue.** Team B's raw-array extractor (built against the confirmed six-array structure) worked on 451/549 real `.ccmesh_pc` files but failed on 98 richer ones, with a brute-force scan finding no valid Mesh-block marker anywhere. Team B named two specific failing files. **Both worked perfectly when extracted and tested directly against the already-documented formula** — version `9` landed exactly where expected on both, first try, no searching, using the exact same array counts and offsets Team B themselves reported. This ruled out any new structural/indirection issue. Handed back the likely root cause (the "BlobA" length-prefixed name-list skip, `spec-geometry-format.md` §4.1.2 finding 2 — easy to miss since it isn't one of the "six arrays") plus exact byte offsets for both files to diff against. Also ran a full batch test of all 549 real files in `characters.vpp_pc` myself: 528/549 (96%) passed, and I characterized the other 21 as "tiny degenerate meshes with zero material-block textures, geometry payload simply absent, likely placeholder/censor-bar meshes." **That characterization was WRONG — see the correction at the end of this section.** New tooling: `scratchpad/batch_test_ccmesh.py` (extracts and structurally validates every `.ccmesh_pc` in an archive in one pass — reusable for the same kind of check against other archives or after Team B's fix).

**Thread B — `.glmesh_pc`'s constructor stub, resolved (quick, as hoped, but with the opposite answer to the hoped-for one).** `LAB_0074c530` turned out to be a **real, substantial function** — Ghidra had never auto-created a function boundary at it only because it's exclusively invoked *indirectly*, through the registration table (the same mechanism documented generically in `spec-resource-dispatch.md`), so normal disassembly-flow analysis never reached it. A function boundary has now been created there in the Ghidra project. **Clean, confirmed answer: `.gcmesh_pc`'s six-array structure does NOT apply to level/static-prop meshes.** `.clmesh_pc`/`.glmesh_pc` has its own distinct magic number (`0x4fe66afa`, not `0x424BD00D`), requires an exact version of `20` (not a range), has a much larger fixed header (`0x140` bytes vs. `0x88`), and delegates to a sequence of ~8 separate sub-parsing functions rather than six gated arrays. **Also corrected, mirroring the original `.ccmesh_pc` correction exactly: this structure lives in `.clmesh_pc` (the small file), not `.glmesh_pc`** — confirmed by tracing the exact buffer register passed to both the material-block validator and this new header parser. Full detail in `spec-geometry-format.md` §4.2; §6 open item #6 marked resolved. The ~8 sub-parser addresses are identified but not individually traced — a real, scoped follow-on if `.clmesh_pc`'s own vertex format ever becomes a priority, not pursued this pass (matches the "don't force it" framing the peer gave — tracing stopped at a clean, conclusive answer rather than continuing open-endedly).

**New Ghidra bookmarks/function boundaries this update:** `LAB_0074c530` (now a real function), `FUN_0074a030` — both commented and confirmed saved.

### 10.1 Correction (2026-09-09): the "21 degenerate meshes" theory was wrong

Team B fixed their BlobA bug and found two more of their own (empty-array padding; and the final step to the Mesh sub-block being 8-byte aligned per §4.1, not 16 — indistinguishable on simple samples). Result on their side: **1803/1803 real files across two independent archives, zero failures.**

They also corrected *my* finding, and they're right — I verified it directly before accepting it. **The 21 files are not degenerate/placeholder meshes.** The real cause is a **mandatory** trailing pad after the material block: when the block's end already lands on a 16-byte boundary, a **full extra 16 bytes** of padding still follow, so the geometry magic sits at `naive_roundUp16 + 16`. A plain round-up-to-16 lands inside that pad, reads zeros, and *looks* like an absent payload. Verified on `oleg_privacybar.ccmesh_pc`: round-up-to-16 → `0x20` reads `00000000`; mandatory rule → `0x30` reads `0x424BD00D`. Applying the fix takes my own batch test from **528/549 to 549/549**. Most of the 21 carry 6–9 real textures; only 3 have zero.

Written up properly in `spec-geometry-format.md` §3.1.1 (new), and the published formula in `spec-geometry-format.md` §4.1.2 finding 1 corrected — it said "16-byte aligned", which a reader implements as round-up-to-16, and that is wrong.

**Lesson, and it belongs next to the §5 hash-sort and §11 byte-replay ones:** *a wrong offset rule produced data that looked like a coherent alternative explanation.* The zeros weren't evidence of an empty payload; they were the pad. I had a real signal (exactly 21 files, all small, all reading zero where a magic should be), built a plausible story that fit it, and reported it as a "minor, already-understood case" — when the honest read was "21 files don't parse and I don't know why." **A tidy explanation for anomalous data is not the same as a verified one; prefer "unexplained" over "explained away."**

## 11. Session Update (2026-09-09) — Registration-Table Inventory Complete

Delivered as **`spec-format-inventory.md`** — the lookup table that makes "what's worth looking at next" a table read instead of a fresh disassembly walk.

**What it contains:** all **43** registered resource types (not "~45" as previously estimated — that estimate is now corrected everywhere it appeared), each with type ID, internal name, per-platform file extensions, constructor and destructor addresses, and whether this project has characterized that format.

**Notable findings beyond the table itself:**

- **Every extension is platform-selected.** What the decompiler rendered as an indirection is an index into a per-platform extension table; reading those tables out of the binary gives `0=pc, 1=ps2, 2=ps3, 3=xbox, 4=xbox2` consistently across all 13 tables. The shipped PC executable still carries every console platform's extension strings.
- **Type IDs 6 and 28 don't exist** — no registration for either anywhere in the function.
- **Five types register no constructor at all** (`Zone (fast)`, `Zone (slow)`, `Buffer`, `Zone header (fast)`, `Zone header (slow)`) — independently confirms and extends `spec-terrain-format.md`'s earlier "zones have no dedicated constructor" finding.
- **New shared-loader relationship found:** `Level Mesh` and `Level Mesh Collision` share one constructor exactly — so a level mesh's collision data is a `.clmesh_pc` file with no GPU-side counterpart. (Adds to the already-known `Character mesh`/`Static Mesh` pair.)
- **A previously-undocumented format pair:** `.cvbl_pc`/`.gvbl_pc` ("Low Mips"), almost certainly the low-resolution half of texture streaming — it shares the PEG destructor and PEG size tables, and the install ships a matching `high_mips.vpp_pc`. Identified, not confirmed; flagged as a clean quick target.
- **A real gotcha:** `Anim File` (21) and `Animation file` (41) both register `.anim_pc` but use completely different loaders. Don't assume one `.anim_pc` parser covers both.

**Methodology note that earned its place in the doc (§5):** the compiler writes each record's name as partial byte writes that deliberately reuse the *previous* entry's leftover bytes. Naive decoding produced two plausible-looking but wrong names (`Pcust meshcvtf` for `Pcust mesh`, `Treel Mesh` for `Tree`) plus three wrong constructor attributions — all readable, all thematically sensible, none obviously wrong in a finished table. Caught only by replaying every write against a simulated buffer instead of reading immediates. Standing lesson: **when reconstructing compiler-emitted data, replay the writes; a plausible decoded string is not evidence the decode was right.**

**Tooling:** `scratchpad/parse_reg_table.py` — replays the registration function's writes and emits the structured inventory. Reusable if the table is ever re-examined. `tools/scripts/DumpExtTables.java` — dumps the per-platform extension/size tables out of the binary.

---

## 12. Session Update (2026-09-09) — `.anim_pc` Animation Format

Target picked from `spec-format-inventory.md` §6. New doc: **`spec-anim-format.md`**.

**Approach:** same dual method that resolved `.rig_pc` — disassembly of both registered constructors plus **bulk statistical analysis of all 4,209 real `.anim_pc` files** in `preload_anim.vpp_pc` (mode-(b), all raw, fully reliable). Claims are stated against the whole population, not a few samples.

**Confirmed:**
- Magic **`ANIM`**, 4,209/4,209.
- **Version byte at `+0x04` must be exactly 14** — the loader rejects anything else, and all 4,209 files read 14. Confirmed from both directions.
- **Flags byte at `+0x05`**; **bit `0x10` gates an optional file-relative offset at `+0x40`** that gets fixed up to a pointer at load (same idiom as `.rig_pc`/`.ccmesh_pc`). Empirically: valid in **167/167** gated files vs **3.4%** in the 4,030 ungated ones — the ungated population is a built-in control group.
- **A root-motion delta transform: a unit quaternion at `+0x0C` (x,y,z,w) plus a translation vec3 at `+0x1C`.** This is the strongest confirmation in the project so far: **4,209/4,209** normalize to 1.0, against **0/4,209** at a shifted offset and **0/12,177** at random offsets. Translation is exactly zero in 1,953 in-place animations (a further 7 within 1e-4) and large in traversal ones — *the 1,954 originally reported was a `<1e-6` threshold artifact, caught by Team B's independent reproduction; corrected in the spec.* Root motion is what makes a character move rather than slide, so this is directly load-bearing for a recomp.
- **The two loaders are one format** — resolving the trap `spec-format-inventory.md` §3 flagged. Both are thin wrappers keying on a **`.animx`** runtime name (authoring-vs-shipped split, like `_cust.xtbl` → `.cvtf_pc`); type 21 addresses by resolved handle, type 41 by name; both reach the same acceptance function. One parser covers both — now traced, not assumed.

**Honest negative:** the **keyframe payload is not resolved**, and the obvious `frames × tracks × bytes_per_key` model is *measurably wrong* — median 0.55 bytes per frame-track with stdev 0.34, i.e. no consistent key size and a nominal half-byte per key. Consistent with sparse/compressed keys (standard for shipped animation) but not decodable by arithmetic. Reaching it needs the runtime *sampling* code, a much larger subsystem — flagged as a deliberate separate target rather than drifted into.

**Tooling correction found by this pass:** `preload_anim.vpp_pc` sets the container shared-stream flag but stores every entry **raw**. Our extractor checked the container flag before the per-entry sentinel and failed outright. **The per-entry raw sentinel must take precedence.** Fixed in `scratchpad/scan_meshes.py`; folded into `spec-vpp-container.md` as new §5.9 with a five-archive survey showing the two signals are independent.

**New Ghidra bookmarks:** `FUN_004bea70` (the shared acceptance/validation function — the key one), `FUN_00731b90` (type 21 ctor), `FUN_00707a00` (type 41 ctor), `FUN_004bf700` (the disk-load path).

**New tooling:** `scratchpad/scan_ext.py` (generic extension scanner for any archive), `scratchpad/anim_bulk.py` (bulk header analysis across a whole archive).

---

## 13. Session Update (2026-09-09) — `.cmorph_pc` Morph-Target Format

Target from `spec-format-inventory.md` §6. New doc: **`spec-morph-format.md`**. Same dual method as `.rig_pc`/`.anim_pc`, against **every shipped `.cmorph_pc`** (2,946 entries, 1,541 unique).

**Proven:** the loader-derived structure — outer `0x1337BEEF`/v5 header, inner `0x0BADBEEF`/v3 **"Morph" block** (its own error string; shared engine infra in the same module as the "Mesh" block), mode field, target directory, 40-byte descriptors, 16-aligned bulk runs of `N x 12`-byte elements, trailing sentinel — **reproduces the exact byte size of 1,541/1,541 files** and the trailing sentinel is present in 1,541/1,541. The **vertex index is the `u16` at element `+6`**: ascending, unique, and equal to the descriptor's declared maximum in **25,789/25,789** records, against a **0.008%** control at `+0` (2 / 25,467 over `N ≥ 2`; the 1.3% first reported was diluted by 322 single-element records that pass "ascending" vacuously — Team B's correction, re-verified 2026-09-10). Team B's replay also reproduces every structural number (1,541/1,541) and adds that **43% of descriptors declare `N = 0`** — a target touching no vertices; structure, not artifact (a full slider set per file with only some active is the hypothesis). Morph targets are **sparse**.

**Zero `.gmorph_pc` files ship** (2,946 vs 0) — fully explained: the block supports GPU-side payload modes 0/2, but every shipped file is inline mode 1. Noted in `spec-resource-dispatch.md` §7: the resolver must tolerate an absent secondary file.

**Three loaders resolved:** type 7 is the only parser (pool slot -> validator -> Morph block -> registration); types 11/12 **don't parse** — they stash name+buffers into a customization singleton. One format, one parser.

**Honest negative:** the 12-byte elements are provably **not** float3 (read as floats they're ~1e38 — a sanity check that killed that reading after exact-size arithmetic had made it look confirmed). Three quantised `u16` components with per-target dequant floats in the descriptor; the **formula is open** and needs the runtime apply-morph code, not the loader.

**Two self-caught errors worth the record** (`spec-morph-format.md` §10): a `(1426,1425)` "header vertex count" from two same-type samples that the population showed was `(1,0)` in 1,246/1,541; and the float3 reading above. Structure and encoding are separate claims and need separate tests.

**Also this session:** Team B independently reproduced every `spec-anim-format.md` statistic against all 4,209 files and caught one — my "1,954 exactly-zero translations" was a `<1e-6` threshold artifact; true count is 1,953 exact (+7 within 1e-4). Verified against my own data and corrected in the spec. Both teams now have validated readers for `.anim_pc`'s confirmed portions.

**New Ghidra bookmarks:** `FUN_0074e500` (outer validator), `FUN_00e71940` (shared Morph block — the key one), `FUN_0074e580` (type-7 chain + pool), and all three constructors.

## 14. Session Update (2026-09-09) — `.ccar_pc` / `.gcar_pc` Vehicle Geometry

Target from `spec-format-inventory.md` §6. New doc: **`spec-vehicle-geometry.md`**. Constructor-first, as directed — and a single population magic-scan showed, before any new structure was read, that every one of the 393 shipped vehicles **embeds the `.ccmesh_pc` sub-header** and 326 embed the **`.cmorph_pc` Morph block**. Two prior specs did most of the work.

**Confirmed (all against the full 393-pair population):**
- Outer header: `u32 0x38` required (393/393); `+0x04` = offset of the embedded mesh region — the full `.ccmesh_pc` chain lands on "Mesh" version 9 in **393/393**, cross-ref equals `.gcar_pc` word 0 in 393/393; `+0x08` = embedded Morph block or `-1` (326 / 67, exact complement); `+0x0C` = an offset **into `.gcar_pc`** for the morph's g-side data (326/326 in range; ≥ `.ccar_pc` size in all).
- **The vehicle morph is mode 0** (326/326) — the GPU-side path no standalone morph used. The mode-0 bookend sentinel sits at exactly the `.gcar_pc` `+0x0C` offset in **326/326 vs 0/2,400** random offsets, and the morph region always follows the vertex buffer. **Closes `spec-morph-format.md` §11 item 7 for mode 0.**
- **Part hierarchy:** count at `+0x390`, 224-byte records at `+0x3A0`. 9,951/9,951 records: 4×4 row-major transform (translation in row 3), printable name, parent index valid (a forest, 14–21 roots typical). Type enum decoded from names: wheels front/rear (+ second/third sets), hinged panels, windows, suspension, steering, wings, rotors.
- **Parts bind to `.rig_pc` bones by name** (plus fixed `camera`/`camtarget`/`camFOV`/`camDOF`); remap array written at header `+0x20`. `.rig_pc` is a hard dependency of vehicles, not just characters.
- **Hardcoded per-vehicle special cases** in the constructor (garbage truck, backhoe, tow truck, muscle04) — behaviour a faithful file reader won't reproduce on its own.
- The material-reference block is **optional here and absent in 393/393**; the loader calls its validator and continues regardless. Materials come from elsewhere — open.

**Open:** five never-null header offsets and several optional ones with uncharacterized targets; the `+0xCC` name-resolved table (likely materials); `+0xE0` records and `+0x134`/`+0x140` arrays; part-record pointer fields and most flag bits; the vehicle g-side vertex offset (assumed `0x10` from the shared code path, not re-traced); the 0xB80-byte global vehicle-info table.

**Cross-document updates:** `spec-morph-format.md` §6/§11 (mode 0 observed), `spec-geometry-format.md` new §4.1.3 (reuse + optional material block), `spec-vehicle-data.md` new §5.1 pointer, inventory row 1.

**Methodology (in the doc, §9):** scan a new format's population for every catalogued magic before assuming it is new.

**New Ghidra bookmarks:** `FUN_00ab1080` (ctor), `FUN_00aaf7c0` (the embed function — the key one), `FUN_00aaf690` (part-record fixups), `FUN_00ab0730` (part→bone binding), `FUN_00aaaea0` (bbox).

**New tooling:** `scratchpad/vehgeo_bulk.py` (population scan + known-magic detector — the reuse detector, reusable for any new format).

---

## 15. Session Update (2026-09-10) — `.rig_pc` Skeleton Format, v2

Approved as §4's top item (it blocks vehicles as well as characters). `spec-rig-format.md` **rewritten as v2** against **585 real rigs** (40 in `preload_rigs.vpp_pc` — including the vehicle rigs — and 545 in `characters.vpp_pc`), from the loader's fixup function plus population tests with controls. Several fields were found by *search* across every record offset rather than assumed.

**The v1 layout was wrong**, and the population said so unambiguously: under the loader-derived layout **22,274/22,274** bone names resolve; under v1's "bones at `+0x50`" **168/22,274** (chance). A `u32`-per-bone table sits before the bone array.

**That table is the engine's filename hash on the lowercased bone name — 22,274/22,274, uppercase 0/22,274.** The rotate-6/XOR hash from `spec-vpp-container.md` §2.2 is engine-wide; header `+0x38` points at this table (585/585); it is what the vehicle loader's bone-by-name lookups scan.

**Bone record (40 B):** name; **model-space** rest position (children sit *higher* than roots — parent-relative offsets never would); a rotation triple (|c| ≤ π in 99.5%; outliers all in vehicle rigs); **parent index** at `+0x20` (tree-valid 585/585, real fan-out); `+0x24` is `-1` in every bone on disk. No quaternion, no matrix in the bone record (searched: 1.2% vs 3% random).

**The "second array" is attachment / IK-target points**: named (`hsattach`, `l_/r_elbow_target{a,b,c}`, `l_/r_knee_target{a,b,c}`, plus per-bone points named after their bone), a parent bone index at `+0x38` (12,480/12,480), a loosely-normalized quaternion at `+0x20` (12,473/12,480 within 10%). Only full body rigs carry them.

**Cross-format:** max rig bone count = **109** = max of `.anim_pc` `+0x0B` — that field is the target rig's bone count (HIGH CONFIDENCE). ~~Vehicles resolve part names via this hash table.~~ **(CORRECTED 2026-09-20, `spec-rig-format.md` §13.1: vehicles resolve part names by a case-insensitive string compare over the bone-name pointers, not via the hash table; no runtime reader of the hash table was found.)**

**Honest opens:** the rotation triple's convention; `+0x2C` (a valid bone index that consistently names the first head/face or finger bone — a bone-LOD boundary is the hypothesis, not a finding); attachment `+0x10`/`+0x30`; the lookup routine itself (not decompiled); `Customization_Rig`.

**Self-correction recorded in the doc (§4):** an intermediate reading of `+0x24` as a second hierarchy link passed every tree test *vacuously* — it is always `-1`. A property that holds for a constant field is not evidence. Now a standing check: before crediting a slot with structure, confirm it varies.

**Cross-document updates:** `spec-vpp-container.md` §2.2 (hash is engine-wide), `spec-anim-format.md` `+0x0B` (bone count, HIGH CONFIDENCE), `spec-vehicle-geometry.md` §6 (hash-table lookup), inventory row 20. **Bookmarks:** `FUN_004d0bb0` corrected, `FUN_004bc880` added (labelled hypothesis), `FUN_00748280` updated.

**Tooling:** `scratchpad/rig_bulk.py` — loader-layout replay + slot-search harness (quaternion / index / string searches with random-offset controls); reusable for any record-structured format.

### 15.1 Addendum (2026-09-10) — the anim↔rig binding check

Approved as the cheap follow-on. Three population tests with controls (`spec-anim-format.md` §6a):
- **`+0x0B` = the target rig's bone count — CONFIRMED by per-character pairing** (`plym`/`plyf` → 69 = `cm_body`/`cf_body`; `brute` → 68; `gangf` → 77; `pml1`/`pfl1` → 65 = `jones`; in-set for 4,163/4,209 clips). **`+0x0A` = the animated subset** (equals `+0x0B` on prop rigs; ~46 of 65–77 on humanoids; varies per clip). Corrects the anim spec's "translation-vs-rotation track counts" guess.
- **Clips carry no bone-name hashes** (2/4,209 vs 0/12,000 control) → binding is **by index, not name**.
- **No explicit index list** (8.7%, scattered) and **no header bitmask** (chance-level, see the §5 lesson) → the index map lives inside the undecoded payload. **Track order vs rig order stays OPEN** — closable only by the runtime sampling code, already listed as §4 item 2.

Net: two specs are now tied together at the header level with per-character evidence, one guess corrected, and the remaining question is precisely located rather than open-ended.

## 16. Session Update (2026-09-10) — `.cfmesh_pc` Foliage Mesh

**Doc:** `spec-foliage-format.md` (new). First of the "untouched registered formats" pass, taken per orchestrator direction after the user's decision **not** to open runtime-subsystem investigations (anim payload, morph dequantisation stay parked as scoped open items).

**What it is:** shared material-reference block (**required** — the ctor aborts without it, unlike vehicles) → the mandatory 16-byte pad → a new outer block `0x0FF1C1A1`/v5 → the shared "Mesh" sub-block (v9) **in inline mode** (flags bit 0 clear, 19/19 — the first real-file observation of the inline path §7's disassembly described) → a count-headed handle table → a runtime slot array → **align 16** → `count` length-prefixed inline **material definitions** (shader hash, texture bindings by name, shader-constant name hashes, `vec4` values) → a `.tga` name table → a trailing 1–3 × 6-float table (LOD/fade distances is a HYPOTHESIS). The replay ends exactly at the file's own `+0x28` offset in 19/19 and every material record consumes exactly its size prefix (33/33).

**The engine-wide string hash is now disassembly-confirmed** (`FUN_00da7890`: fold `A–Z` to lowercase, then `rotl32(h,6) XOR byte`). It was inferred for `.vpp_pc` filenames (§2.2) and proven empirically on 22,274 rig bone names (§15); the foliage ctor calls it to register each mesh in a **64-slot** global table — a hard cap.

**Negatives worth keeping:** the texture binding's second `u32` is **not** the filename's engine hash (0/79) — the binder hands it to the shader's slot lookup, so it names the sampler slot. Only two material shapes ship — (3 textures, 9 constants, 11 `vec4`) ×23 and (1, 1, 3) ×10 — and only two shader hashes across all 33 records (one for plants, one for litter).

**Population caveat, stated plainly:** 19 files, one bundle (`sr3_city_0` / `awld_compact`). Every "19/19" is the complete population, not a sample — but there is no second archive to cross-check against, so a bundle-specific quirk would be invisible.

**Cross-document:** inventory row 37 + §6; `spec-rig-format.md` §5 and `spec-vpp-container.md` §2.2 (hash → disassembly-confirmed); `spec-terrain-format.md` §2 (`.fmeshx` → `.cfmesh_pc`); `spec-geometry-format.md` §4.1.2 item 6 (inline mode now observed). **Bookmarks:** 8 (`FOLIAGE_FORMAT` ×7, `ENGINE_HASH` ×1 at `FUN_00da7890`) via `tools/scripts/AddFoliageBookmarks.java`; decompile dumps in `tools/foliage_ctor.txt`, `tools/foliage_parser.txt`, `tools/e71910.txt`.

**Tooling:** `scratchpad/foliage_bulk.py` — the loader replay (material block → pad → outer block → Mesh sub-block length → handle table → slot array → align 16 → material records → `+0x28` landing check), plus the population characterisation and the engine-hash implementation with the 0/79 sampler-slot negative.

**Next (orchestrator's list):** `.csc_pc` (24), `.csrt_pc`/`.gsrt_pc` (36), `.ctdg_pc` (42), `.cvbl_pc`/`.gvbl_pc` (17) — same recipe: registration row → constructor → magic-scan for known sub-blocks → replay against the population with exact-size and offset-landing checks.

## 17. Session Update (2026-09-10) — `.csrt_pc` / `.gsrt_pc` Tree Format

**Doc:** `spec-tree-format.md` (new). Second and last item of the untouched-formats pass before the hold requested by the orchestrator (user decision to stop the teams after current work).

**What it is:** material-reference block → mandatory pad → `'TREE'` block (`0x54524545`, version 204, fixed `0x208`: capsule count at `+0x18`, a per-tree float parameter table at `+0x3C…`) → align 16 → `0x170` geometry sub-structure (bounds min/max as padded `float4`s, **4 LOD slots**, material count) → a **shared material-set block** (`FUN_00e40210`: `0x20` header, names at `+0x20` padded-to-16 **plus one byte**, `count × 8` slots, then foliage's inline material records) → a generic index list (identity) → 4 presence entries (nonzero = a Mesh sub-block follows) → one **g-backed Mesh sub-block** per present LOD (25 total) → three runtime arrays → align 16 → **collision capsules** (`0x60` each: start/end `float4`, radius; 0 on shrubs, 4 on the largest tree). Replay lands exactly on EOF **11/11**; material records exact **37/37**.

**The `g`-file is per-mesh segments:** each Mesh block's check value is the first `u32` of its contiguous `.gsrt_pc` segment (25/25), with the index buffer at segment `+0x10` — the `.gcmesh_pc` offset-`0x10` finding generalised. The Mesh block also ends with a copy of its check value (bookend, 25/25) — added to `spec-geometry-format.md` as item 11.

**Cross-format confirmation of the sampler-slot reading:** the three slot hashes shared by trees and foliage bind diffuse / normal / specular identically in both (185 + 79 bindings, disjoint textures; 0/185 and 0/79 against the engine hash). Two tree-only slots are always bound to placeholders (`flat-normalmap_n`, `missing-alpha`).

**Population caveat:** 11 distinct trees — every one of the 28 shipped copies is byte-identical to its namesake, so this *is* the population; one shader hash and one material shape across all of it.

**Leads recorded, not pursued (hold):** the material-record parser has 10 call sites in 8 functions and the Mesh wrapper 8 — both lists in `tools/tree_callers.txt` map straight onto the remaining untraced formats; the material-set block has two other callers (`FUN_00866d80`, `FUN_00e3e590`).

**Cross-document:** inventory row 36 + §6; `spec-geometry-format.md` §4.1.2 item 11; `spec-foliage-format.md` §3 (handle table = index list), §6.1 (cross-format slots), §8. **Bookmarks:** 6 new (`TREE_FORMAT` ×4, `SHARED_BLOCK` ×2) + 2 appended (`FUN_00e71090`, `FUN_00e718b0`). **Tooling:** `scratchpad/tree_bulk.py` (population/magic scan), `tree_peek.py`, `tree_replay.py` (the replay), `tree_extra.py` (g-segmentation, block constancy, capsule and slot checks); Ghidra dumps `tools/tree_ctor.txt`, `tools/tree_subparsers.txt`, `tools/tree_callers.txt`.
