the translation decoder is whatever reads the *other* cursors the setup function
writes (`+0x2b0`/`+0x2b4`/`+0x2b8`/`+0x2bc`/`+0x2d0`). Binary-wide that predicate
matched **461** functions — the same non-selectivity as §26.12's displacement scan,
and expected this time. Restricted to the subsystem's code range it left **three**,
of which exactly one touches all five translation cursors and none of the rotation
ones. **A predicate that is useless alone becomes decisive when intersected with a
range derived from data layout rather than guessed** — which is the §26.12 lesson
used forward instead of relearned.

**New harness:** `anim_trans.py`. **New Ghidra scripts:** `FindAnimTrans.java`,
`DumpTransConsts.java`.


### 26.14 `.anim_pc` rotation arithmetic — replayed, and the first oracle was vacuous

`spec-anim-format.md` §6c.2. The rotation per-sample arithmetic finally got a
numeric replay. **The headline is the methodology, because the obvious oracle
failed in a way that would have looked like success.**

The intended test: a smallest-three triple must satisfy `x²+y²+z² ≤ 1`, and the
decoder clamps that argument, so breaches should be absent. Over 2,963,687 samples
the real reading breaches at **0.001%** — which is excellent in isolation and
**means almost nothing**, because the byte-shifted controls breach at only
**0.002–0.058%**. Most stored components are small, so the constraint could rarely
have failed. **This is the half-float `abs(u) < 64` screen again** (§6.1): a test
that passes because it cannot fail, scoring well on data it does not discriminate.
Second instance of that exact trap in this project, and the reason a control is not
optional.

Restricting the clamp test to samples whose base term is already large (n=129,261),
and adding an intra-run smoothness test — consecutive keys share a base, so they
must move continuously:

| Test | real | samples +1 | samples +2 | records +2 |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| restricted clamp breach | **0.03%** | 0.12% | 0.28% | 1.89% |
| jitter median | **0.0193** | 0.0497 | 0.0635 | 0.0442 |
| jitter p90 | **0.0663** | 0.2155 | 0.1989 | 0.1436 |

**Reported as PARTIAL, not as a confirmation.** 2.6–3.3× separation is real but is
not the order-of-magnitude separation this project's structural results show, and a
byte-shifted control is intrinsically weak here because a shifted read still lands
on real packed 6-bit fields. The tests show the reading is bounded and smooth —
which several nearby readings would also be. Status: **[HIGH CONFIDENCE — inferred;
replay consistent, controls weak]**. A genuine step, not a graduation.

One incidental consistency check is worth more than it looks: the base term alone
spans **±0.7072 = ±1/√2**, exactly a smallest-three component's range. The scale
constant and the field widths corroborate each other with no test involved.

**New harness:** `anim_rot.py` — which **keeps the vacuous first test in place**
rather than deleting it, so the weak version cannot be rediscovered and quoted as
though it were the evidence.


### 26.15 Vehicle loader — three implementation-team questions answered

`spec-vehicle-geometry.md` §7.2. Ranked as they were asked.

**Q1, the vehicle material region: there is no vehicle-specific walk.** The embed
function hands the cursor to **the full `.ccmesh_pc` machinery, same function,
unmodified**. The reference-block validator is called but returns absent in
**393/393** vehicles and its result is ignored — the documented reason vehicles carry
no reference block. **So "do vehicle material sub-records use the length-prefixed
idiom?" is not a vehicle question**; it is the same question about `.ccmesh_pc`, and
an answer for either carrier transfers by construction. That collapses their problem
via *identical code path* rather than *same idiom reused*, which is stronger — it
assumes no similarity. **[CONFIRMED — disassembly.]** Explicitly **not** answered:
the advance rule *inside* the material set, because that validator skips the block by
its declared length and never enumerates sub-records.

**Q2, per-part material scoping: not established.** Part records carry eight
fixed-up pointer fields with several undocumented, so it is structurally possible and
the candidates are enumerable — but nothing read shows a material count or index in a
part record. **[OPEN.]** Their reasoning for why it would explain `+0x0C`'s
upper-bound behaviour is sound and kept as a hypothesis, not promoted.

**Q3, vehicle body textures: YES, from a runtime catalogue. [CONFIRMED —
disassembly.]** `.cvtf_pc` is a separately registered resource type with its own
constructor, registered in the same table as `.ccar_pc` but independent of it, and
registered **twice** as distinct vehicle and character variants. Independently
corroborated by a separate paint subsystem with named colour slots in functions
unrelated to any geometry loader. **Generic-looking vehicle body textures are a
confirmed NON-BUG** — the mesh is not the source of truth for those surfaces. Moves
§7.1's paint question from HYPOTHESIS to CONFIRMED for the *source*.

**A documentation gap that was mine.** The `.ccar_pc` registered constructor was in
this file and in the format inventory but **missing from `spec-vehicle-geometry.md`**,
which cited only the *mesh* constructor — so a reader of the vehicle spec was one
indirection from the vehicle entry point. Every question above was answered by
reading that constructor's own call list. **A correct fact in the wrong document
makes a question look harder than it is.**

**Third authorization precedent, recorded because the pattern is the point.** A
relayed verbatim user quote — that both teams should be able to work without
intervention — was **held** against the three parked §25.2 items, consistent with the
two earlier holds (§26.6). The standing reason applies: a relay is not the user typing
in this session. The sharper reason is specific to this wording: **a message saying
"you should not need me" cannot authorise the things that specifically need them.** If
an item is parked because it requires the user's direct decision, a general statement
about reducing intervention restates the problem rather than resolving it. What it
does endorse is continuing *already-authorised* work without checking in — which is
why these three questions were answered rather than asked about.


### 26.16 The material-set advance rule — resolved for both carriers at once

`spec-vertex-format.md` §8.4. The question §26.15 left explicitly open, closed one
level deeper in the same call chain.

**It is neither of the two options the question posed.** The material region is
**fixed-stride descriptor arrays** — `count × 8` then `count × 4`, each preceded by an
8-byte alignment, with counts at sub-header `+0x0C` (the material count) and `+0x0E`
(**a second count not previously documented**) — followed by a **variable-length
per-material loop** running exactly `u16(+0x0C)` times. **No length-prefixed
sub-record headers exist at the array level**, so any greedy segmentation of that
region is solving a non-problem; the counts give the boundaries. **[CONFIRMED —
disassembly.]** And by §26.15's identical-code-path result, this lands on `.ccar_pc`
unchanged — one read, both carriers, exactly as intended.

**It also reframes one of this project's own records.** §26.9 wrote `+0x0C` as
*"exact on meshes, only an upper bound on vehicles"*. `+0x0C` **drives the
per-material loop**, so it is the exact record count on **both**. What is not exact on
vehicles is the relation between materials and **draw runs** — a different quantity.
Runs reference materials, several runs may share one, and a material need not be
referenced at all, so `runs ≤ count` one-sided is the correct expectation and
equality on meshes was a coincidence of the data. **The measurement was right and the
frame was wrong** — the third time this session that has been the shape of the error
(cf. §26.12's diluted denominator, §26.13's inherited-frame correction).

The implementation team had independently measured the one-sided relation with their
guard removed — runs `<` count in 369/372, `==` in 3/372, **never `>`** — which is
stronger evidence for a bound than an average, and it agrees with the loop structure.

**A trap they found that belongs in the spec, not just in their code.** Relaxing an
equality guard to `≤` is not sufficient: a name check accepting "any printable string
with a dot" also accepts **mid-name offsets**, because the blob is NUL-separated — so
a pointer landing inside `T_bulldog_D1B.tga` yields `_bulldog_D1B.tga` or a bare
`.tga`, all passing. Characters measure 100.00% name-at-boundary against vehicles'
99.44%. **~1% impurity is ~99 wrong bindings, and 99%-correct renders fine** — the
silently-plausible class again. `spec-vertex-format.md` §8.4.2 now states the two structural requirements:
the offset must land on a name boundary (blob start is a **leading NUL**, so offset 0
is not a name), and the sampler hash must be a full 32-bit constant (under 256, or
`-1`, is not a hash).

**And their broken control is the most transferable thing in this exchange.** Their
second control shifted the name base by one byte and returned **byte-identical**
results to the real scan — 9,878 runs both ways — because a 1-byte shift merely
produces different truncations that their name check accepted equally. Had that been
their only control, *"two controls agree"* would have read as confirmation. **A
control that agrees exactly with the real measurement is reporting that the control
is broken, not that the measurement is right** — and exact agreement, not mere
closeness, is the tell. Now a §5 lesson; it is the inverse of §26.14's vacuous
oracle, where the *test* could not fail, and here the *control* could not fail.


**Postscript — the predicate was a correctness check, and the guard was wrong in two
directions at once.** The implementation team promoted their name-start test from a
measurement predicate into their reader's name validator. Neutral where things
already worked (characters 546/546 located, 6,064/6,064 cross-checked, suite green),
but **2 of the 3 previously "successfully bound" vehicles flipped to rejected** — each
had a truncated name accepted as real, i.e. **live wrong bindings presenting as
correct on the exact path a renderer trusts**. Their binding count goes 3/372 → 1/372,
which is a bug *fix*. (The one-sided relation above is unaffected: it was measured
with the guard removed.)

Their generalisation, which is the transferable part: **strictness is not a scalar.**
The old exact-run-count guard was **too tight in the dimension everyone was watching**
— does it accept enough vehicles, the visible "why only 3 of 372?" — and **wide open
in a dimension nobody was watching**, accepting a name carved from the middle of
another string. The too-tight half generates complaints; the too-loose half generates
renders that look fine. **The loud failure hides the quiet one.** Now a §5 lesson.

They also deliberately left their *validation harness's* own scan untightened, still
reporting the raw pre-fix numbers, so the measuring instrument does not track the
thing it measures. Worth naming as practice: **when you maintain a reader and its
validator together, a fix applied to both erases the signal the validator exists to
produce.**


### 26.17 A published relation was backwards — and the correction nearly repeated the error

`spec-vertex-format.md` §8.4.1 of `spec-vertex-format.md` asserted **`runs ≤ materialCount`** and cited the
clean team's *369 `<` / 3 `==` / 0 `>`* to support it. **That figure counts BINDING
runs; the section is about DRAW runs.** Different quantities, and the substitution
inverts the conclusion. Re-measured here over every `.ccar_pc` (393 located, 90,290
draw ranges):

| Relation to `materialCount` | `<` | `==` | `>` |
|---|---|---|---|
| **draw range count** | 1 | 0 | **392** |
| distinct **masked** ids (`field & 0xFFFF`) | **393** | 0 | 0 |

**Draw ranges exceed `materialCount` in 392/393 — the opposite of what was
published.** The bound holds for *distinct materials referenced* once the id is
masked per §8.2, at **393/393**, which extends that section's existing 86/86 to the
full population. So the intended claim was true of the right quantity and attached to
the wrong one.

**The tell was inside the paragraph.** Its own premise — *"a run references a
material, several may share one, a material need not be referenced"* — predicts draw
runs **≥** distinct materials used. **The prose argued against its own conclusion and
the citation papered over the gap.** A number borrowed from another team arrives
without the definition that made it true.

**And the correction nearly shipped a second error.** Comparing the draw-range id
**unmasked** shows distinct ids *exceeding* `materialCount` in 385/393 — meaningless,
because the field is **packed 16:16**, a trap this project documented in §26.9 and
which I walked straight into *while fixing a different error in the same section*.
Masked, it reverses to 393/393. I also discarded `max(field >> 16) < materialCount`
at 393/393 as **near-vacuous**: the high half runs 0–14 against a median
`materialCount` of 44, so it could barely have failed — the §26.14 lesson applied to
my own supporting evidence rather than to someone else's.

**Nothing else moves.** The `+0x0C`-drives-the-loop result (§26.16) rests on
disassembly, not on this statistic, and stands. So does the stronger structural point
that counts can never recover the association. The association's leading handle
remains what §8.2 already flagged — the **high half** of the packed id — together with
the unread per-material helper (§27.2).

**New harness:** `car_runcount.py`.


### 26.18 Vehicle material sets are paired — found by measuring the slack instead of the bound

`spec-vertex-format.md` §8.4.3. **The clean team caught that my replacement claim in
§26.17 was itself near-vacuous** — the same test I had applied to my own high-half
number one paragraph earlier, and missed on the headline. *"Distinct masked ids <
materialCount in 393/393"*: **ask what a failure would look like** — a draw range
naming an invalid index, i.e. a corrupt file. So it means *"no vehicle has an
out-of-range material id"*. A real invariant, no evidence about the association, and
never tight (0/393 files at zero slack).

**Their better idea — measure the slack, not the bound — produced a real finding.**

| | characters | vehicles |
|---|---|---|
| files | 549 | 393 |
| mean slack (declared − referenced) | **0.0** | **21.6** |
| files with zero slack | **549 / 549** | **0 / 393** |
| slack as share of declared | **0.0%** | **49.9%** |
| `materialCount == 2 × referenced` | — | **351 / 393** (+36 at `2k−1`) |
| **maximum ratio observed** | 1.0 | **exactly 2.000** |

**Vehicles declare exactly twice the materials they draw, and the ratio never exceeds
2.** Characters declare exactly what they draw, 549/549. Referenced ids are a dense
prefix in both, so the unreferenced vehicle materials are a contiguous suffix.
**[CONFIRMED — empirical, with a control population at ratio 1.0.]**

**It explains §26.9's "upper bound" once and for all.** `+0x0C` is an exact count of
*declared* materials on both carriers (§26.16, disassembly). What differs is that
**vehicles declare 2× what they draw** — so every count-matching candidate was being
compared against a quantity guaranteed to miss by a factor of two. Six refutations at
chance, against a number that could never have matched. The "upper bound on vehicles"
framing is retired on both sides.

**But this does NOT revive counting as a route to the association** — a distinction the
clean team asked to have held explicitly, and they were right to, because my own
phrasing invited the misreading. Both are true simultaneously: the six candidates
failed for a now-explained reason, **and** counts remain incapable of yielding an
association. **A corrected count tells you HOW MANY, never WHICH.** The operative
statement is the earlier, principled one — not recoverable from counts at all — and it
should not be softened by the better diagnosis. **A satisfying mechanical explanation
for why an approach failed reads as a licence to retry it; here it is only a
correction to the post-mortem.**

**Independently confirmed:** separate population (372 vehicles), separate tooling,
**336/372 = 90.3%** at exactly 2× against my 351/393 = 89.3%, same **2.0000** cap never
exceeded, control at **exactly 1.0000** across 549 files. Their `== 2× − 1` bucket reads
272/549 on characters, which is a **degenerate-test artifact** at one material
(`d == d` and `d + 1 == 2d` both trivially hold), not a contradiction — flagged here
because an unexplained number sitting beside a clean result is what a future reader
tries to reconcile as real. My control is slack-based and has no such degeneracy.

**What the second half is remains [HYPOTHESIS]** — a paired variant per surface, with
damage state the natural candidate, doing no work in the conclusion. And it is now
the **best lead on the association**: ~22 records per vehicle exist for a reason that
is not "a draw range points at me", so if the per-material record holds an
association key, that half is where evidence for what it keys on is most visible.

**My SEARCH was not the problem but my GUARDS were — corrected here after
reconciliation.** §8.3 documents no anchor to have missed: the binding run's start
offset **varies per record** (`+0x64`, `+0x04`, `+0x74`, …) and must be located by its
own constraint, so a sliding search is correct and the clean team's is sliding too.
**But I wrote that the gap "lies elsewhere" in a way that implied my detector was
sound, and it was not** — the entire 2× was content validation I had omitted,
including the hash guard `spec-vertex-format.md` §8.4.2 specifies. The search was right; the acceptance rule
was too permissive. A tempting
near-match also died here: my median of 48 runs/vehicle against a median
`materialCount` of 44 suggested one binding run per declared material — **per file it
scattered 257 `>` / 110 `<` / 26 `==`**, so there is no such relation and the medians
agreeing meant nothing. My speculation that the clean team's lower count might be
finding only the *referenced* half is therefore unsupported and withdrawn.

**A probe on the pairing hypothesis was attempted and its output DISCARDED — recorded
so nobody repeats it.** The obvious test of "the unreferenced half is a damage variant"
is to look at which textures the binding runs name. Scanning the post-Mesh region for
12-byte binding entries found **440,086 runs across 393 vehicles — 1,119 per vehicle**,
against the clean team's ~26. **427,572 of those were length 1**: a single entry passing
"offset lands in the blob, index equals 0" occurs constantly by chance, so the scan was
measuring noise and its name histogram was worthless (`al_grime.tga` "bound" 135,325
times across 393 files). Requiring length ≥ 2 gave 19,240 runs — still roughly **2×** the
clean team's 9,878 on a comparable population, an unexplained discrepancy. **No rate
from this is published.** `burn_test.tga` and `shd_damagenormal_n.tga` are the two
most-bound vehicle textures under every threshold tried, which is *consistent* with
damage variants, but those names were already documented in §26.10 and a name census is
not a pairing measurement. **The damage reading stays [HYPOTHESIS — unconfirmed]**; the
2× pairing itself is unaffected, since it rests on draw-range ids and `+0x0C`, not on
binding runs.

**SEVEN of my own results were demoted, discarded or withdrawn across §26.17–26.18,
against one real finding.** Enumerated, because I quoted this tally as "three" here,
"four" in one relay and "five" in another before checking it — **a count of one's own
retracted claims is exactly the number that must not drift**, and it drifted three ways
inside a thread whose whole subject was numbers drifting:

1. `runs ≤ materialCount` — published **backwards**; draw ranges exceed it in 392/393.
2. "distinct ids exceed `materialCount` in 385/393" — a **packed-field artefact**, the
   §26.9 trap I had documented myself.
3. `max(field >> 16) < materialCount` at 393/393 — **near-vacuous**, caught by me.
4. "distinct masked ids < `materialCount`" at 393/393 — **near-vacuous**, caught by the
   clean team; demoted to a weak invariant.
5. The binding-run scan: 440,086 runs (97% length 1), and the 19,240 retry — **both
   discarded**, no rate published. (Now *explained*, see below — still not republished.)
6. "one binding run per declared material" from medians 48 vs 44 — **withdrawn**;
   scattered 257/110/26 per file.
7. "their locator is probably anchored and mine is the naive one" — **wrong in both
   halves**: their scan is sliding too, and mine was the under-validated one.

**The one finding that survived — the 2× material pairing — came from the clean team
asking what a failing case would have looked like**, not from any number I published.
That ratio is what the work actually looked like, and it belongs in the record at its
true value rather than a flattering or a self-flagellating one.

**The 2× scan discrepancy is now fully reconciled, and the cause was mine.** Not the
search strategy — both scans slide `+4` from the Mesh block end and test every
candidate — and not the population, which is the *same* 393 files, not merely
comparable. **It was content validation.** Same search, same length ≥ 2, same files:

| Guards applied | Runs |
|---|---|
| clean team's (non-zero hash **and** name resolves to a printable dotted string) | **10,359** |
| mine (offset lands in the blob, slot indices sequential) | 19,240 |
| none at all | 27,050 |

My figure **brackets between theirs and no-validation**, so my detector validates
something, just less. **And the guard I was missing is the one `spec-vertex-format.md` §8.4.2 of
`spec-vertex-format.md` specifies — a section I wrote in this same session**: the
sampler hash must be a full 32-bit constant, since a value under 256 or `-1` is not a
hash and is the cheapest available rejection. **My own tool did not apply my own
document's rule.** Same shape as §26.15's missing constructor address: the fact was
right, in the right place, and not reaching the thing that needed it.

**Which figure is CORRECT is a separate question from what differs, and the bracket
does not settle it** (the clean team's own caution). A smaller, more conservative
count is not right merely for being stricter — the bracket localises the
disagreement to content validation and nothing more. **The case for their guard rests
on its own evidence:** a character control at 546/546 located and 6,064/6,064
cross-checked with a 100.00% name-start oracle, plus a direct demonstration that
loosening it admits real garbage — the truncation family, and 226 entries whose
"hash" reads under 256 or `-1`. On that evidence `10,359` is the trustworthy figure.
On the bracket alone it would not be.


### 26.19 `.cmorph_pc` — first authorised pass: architecture, not yet the formula

`spec-morph-format.md` §12. **Reporting an incomplete result deliberately.** The
dequantisation formula is not resolved. Two dead ends were closed and the consumer chain
was mapped with addresses, which that document previously lacked entirely.

**1. The identity search returned a clean negative.** Over 56,822 descriptors / 170,466
axis samples, none of `large/small` constant, `large == small × max|q|`, or
`large == small × 32767` holds. **And §3.5's characterisation needed qualifying: the
components are signed but NOT small** — `max|q| ≥ 32,000` in **136,804 of 170,466 axis
samples**, so the full `int16` range is in use and the decode is not small-delta-times-
scale. Predicate stated so the negative stays re-openable.

**2. The GPU-side class is mode 0/2 only — dead code for every shipped file.** The outer
validator hands the block through **vtable `+0x10`** (vtable `0x012a09cc`). Ghidra had no
functions at three slots, since the only references are indirect; created and read all
four. **Every one gates on `mode == 0 || mode == 2`**, and the buffer-binding slot uses
stride **`0xC` or `8`** — exactly the documented mode-0/2 strides. Every shipped file is
mode 1, and zero `.gmorph_pc` ship, so **this class should not be implemented at all**.
§7's instruction to look at "the GPU-side object the validator hands the block to" was
aimed at the wrong object.

**3. The mode-1 chain, now with addresses.** Type 11/12 stash into customization
singleton **`DAT_0263e0f4`** (buffers at `+0xac..+0xb8` and `+0x100..+0x10c`, names at
`+0x68`/`+0xbc`) **[CORRECTED 2026-09-20, `spec-morph-format.md` §16.1: those offsets are types 10 (`+0x68`/`+0xac..`) and 11 (`+0xbc`/`+0x100..`); type 12 is `+0x15c`/`+0x1a0`/`+0x1a4`]**, and **`FUN_009f36d0`** is the only function touching all eight slots —
the customization asset assembler, which also drives the material-block validator and
the mesh machinery. It is **not** the deformer: 14 float ops and **no int-to-float**.

**Next pass:** the kernel is a callee of `FUN_009f36d0`. Signature required by the data:
int-to-float, stride 12, multiply against a descriptor float at `+0x08`/`+0x14`.
`FUN_004bcb30`/`FUN_004bcca0`/`FUN_004bca00` are the cheapest first look.

**And I burned a pass on the predicate I have now failed with four times today** — a
whole-binary scan for "stride `0xC` + int-to-float + float multiply" returned **272
functions** ranked by size. I ran it *after* writing the §5 lesson that says not to.
**Knowing a lesson and applying it under momentum are different things**, which is worth
recording as its own observation rather than as another instance of the lesson.

**New harness:** `morph_dequant.py`. **New Ghidra scripts:** `FindMorphApply.java`,
`FindMorphVtable.java`, `ReadMorphApplier.java`, `FindPcustMorph.java`,
`ReadPcustCtors.java`, `ReadMorphConsumer.java`.


### 26.20 The rotation refutation did not replicate — and the cause was a contradiction I wrote

The clean team, authorised by the user on `.anim_pc` directly, built a reader from `spec-anim-format.md` §6c
Two tiers reproduced exactly: the **walk** (4,086/4,209, same 123 failures, same flags-
`0x40` split) and **translation** (86,051 records, their +2 control p90 88.22 against my
88.2). **Rotation they reported as decisively refuted** by a ceiling argument: their
assembly caps each component near 0.181, giving a hard maximum of ~36.5° of rotation,
while real animation needs ±180°.

**I measured it before accepting it, and the refutation does not apply to the published
formula.** My harness and `spec-anim-format.md` §6c.2's pseudocode agree exactly; over 2,963,687 real samples
they give **max |component| 0.8785 against a theoretical ceiling of 0.884**, and
rotation angles of **median 31.16°, p90 80.40°, p99 105.10°, max 180.00°**, with
**43.2% of keys past 36.5°**. So the published arithmetic is not subject to their
ceiling — their implementation diverges from it.

**The divergence is my defect, and I found it by looking for it.** `spec-anim-format.md` §6c.2's *prose* said
the per-axis step index comes from the **sample** bytes; its *pseudocode* and the
disassembly both take it from the **control record** bytes. Worse, the prose reading is
**unimplementable**: the delta packing already consumes the top bits of sample byte 0,
so sourcing the step index there double-allocates them. **An implementer resolving that
conflict by reserving those bits for the step index gets a narrower delta and a lower
ceiling** — which is exactly the shape of what they hit. Fixed at source, plus an
exhaustive bit-allocation table so the ambiguity cannot recur, plus the achievable-range
figure stated in the section itself.

**They also found a second notational defect of mine** that cost them 105/4,209 on
their first attempt: `spec-anim-format.md` §6c.1 wrote `align2(p+1)` and `align4(p+3)`, meaning the C idioms
`(p+1) & ~1` and `(p+3) & ~3`, i.e. *round p up*. Read as "call align on the
already-offset value" it applies the offset twice. Rewritten as `alignUp2(p)` with the
definition inline. **Two ambiguities in one section, both found only because someone
implemented from it** — a spec that reads clearly to its author is not thereby
unambiguous, and the only test is an independent implementation.

**Their stratification is better than my aggregate and is adopted:** the walk is
**100.00%** on every flags population except `0x0C`/`0x1C` (0/2 each), which is a
cleaner statement than my 99.89%, since it names the residual instead of smearing it.
*(Reported by them; not independently re-measured here.)*

**Status change:** `spec-anim-format.md` §6c.2's per-sample arithmetic stays **[HIGH CONFIDENCE — inferred]**
and is **not** downgraded to refuted — the refutation was of a different assembly. But
it is not upgraded either: two independent readings of my own section produced different
decoders, which is itself evidence the section was underspecified rather than evidence
the arithmetic is right.


### 26.21 Rotation CONFIRMED — the refutation was a missing factor, and both my predictions were wrong

The rotation refutation resolved: **the defect was in the other implementation, not the
published formula.** Every one of their intermediates matched my worked example exactly
— base `8, -1, -1`; mult `4, 4, 2`; delta `0, 6, -1`, full 6 bits throughout — and the
divergence was the assembly itself: they computed `(base + mult*delta) * SCALE`, dropping
**both** the `*64` on the base and the overall `*4`. Solving my three worked components
backwards recovers the correct expression exactly. After fixing it they get **max
180.00°, median 34.40°, p90 83.69°** against my independent **180.00° / 31.16° / 80.40°**.

**`spec-anim-format.md` §6c.2's per-sample arithmetic is therefore upgraded to [CONFIRMED — disassembly +
empirical].** Two independently-written decoders agreeing on real data is the
confirmation the weak byte-shift controls could not give (§26.14). The residual median
gap (34.40 vs 31.16) is unexplained, small, and recorded rather than smoothed.

**Both of my predictions were wrong, and I am logging that plainly.** The first — agree
on base, diverge at mult or delta — was wrong. The second, which I presented as
arithmetic rather than a guess — a 4-bit base, inferred by solving 0.181 backwards and
landing on exactly 0.176798 — was **also wrong**. The 0.176798 coincidence was real and
meant nothing: it is simply the axis-0 base term of the key I happened to pick. **An
exact numeric coincidence felt like evidence and was not**, which is the medians lesson
(§26.18) in a new costume: a single matching value is an even weaker aggregate than a
median. Two wrong predictions, one real fix, and the fix came from publishing the
intermediates rather than from either prediction.

**The gap they identified was real even though the formula existed.** The assembly
expression was in `spec-anim-format.md` §6c.2 — **85 lines below** the description of the decode, inside the
numeric-replay subsection. They read the description, the bit-allocation table and the
achievable range, and never reached it. **A formula present but far from the description
it belongs to is functionally absent**, and stating the achievable extreme is no
substitute: the factors 64 and 4 are visible inside `(16×32 + 32×64)×4×SCALE` without
being placeable. Hoisted to the top of the section as the first thing stated, with the
reason recorded in place.

**Their own candour is worth carrying.** All three of their rotation-correctness oracles
scored perfectly under the wrong formula — vacuously, because components capped near
0.18 make smallest-three reconstruction and clamping unable to fail. And the tell was
present: **their continuity control scored HIGHER than the real reading**, the exact
"control agrees or inverts" signature from §26.16 that they had written themselves and
applied correctly to their translation control minutes earlier. Their new rule, adopted
here: **verify before refuting, and suspect your own reading first** — going from
"something is wrong" straight to "the spec is wrong" skips the cheaper and more likely
candidate.


### 26.22 Zone streaming subsystem opened — the category table; and an overclaim of mine withdrawn

`spec-world-streaming.md` §7. Third parked item, opened under the user's direct
authorisation (§27.3). **The subsystem registers a fixed table of named streaming
categories at startup** — each a small stack struct `{u32 id, const char *name, …}`
handed to one of **seven** ~147-byte registration helpers. **41 calls, 40 ids spanning
`0x01`–`0x29` (38 distinct), 34 names.** The function had been explicitly left unopened
by the 2026-09-10 ctorless pass.

Ids and names are literals and the name always sits 4 bytes after the id in the same
struct, so the pairing is **exact, not inferred**. Four categories bear directly on zone
loading: **`0x1A` zone always loaded**, **`0x28` zone header cache**, **`0x1F`
interiors**, and the paired **`0x1C` medium lod / `0x1D` high lod level allocators**. A
category set that separates headers from content and medium from high LOD sits
consistently alongside the resource table's `Zone (fast)`/`Zone (slow)` split being a
**loading policy, not a format difference**. **[CONFIRMED — disassembly.]**

**Two ids register twice** (`0x19`, `0x1E`), each through a *different* helper — so the
helpers are not one-per-category and likely distinguish kind of registration. `0x14`,
`0x22`, `0x23` never appear. Both **[OPEN]**.

**Budgets are deliberately NOT published.** The struct carries further immediates that
look like capacities, and my first extraction of them was **discarded**: it paired "most
recent string" with "last small number" and cross-associated fields across adjacent
registrations, reporting an allocation belonging to `cloth sim runtime cpu` against
`time of day`. Attributing them needs the helper bodies read. Separately, 15 *directly
readable* named-pool calls do give exact sizes, and those are published.

**A `.czn_pc` negative worth having:** the `0xFFFB`-family word that stops the refuted
chunk walk is **not literal-checked anywhere in the binary** — every `0xFFFA`–`0xFFFE`
immediate is an unrelated bitmask. **So it is not a sentinel or terminator, it is
ordinary data being misread**, consistent with the walk having left the top level.

### 26.22a My own "end to end" overclaim, withdrawn

The orchestrator corrected their summary that the anim payload was "done"; **the same
overclaim was in my record and is now withdrawn.** §26.13's title and §27.1 both said the
payload was decoded "end to end". The accurate statement: **decode confirmed for the
87.7% of clips that land on their declared end**, with the 518 flags-`0x40` carriers and
the 123 walk failures still unaccounted, plus `spec-anim-format.md` §6c.6's open questions. A good place, not a
finished one. **I wrote "end to end" about work whose residual I had myself measured at
518 + 123 clips two sections earlier** — the phrase was reach, not a slip.

**New from the clean team on flags `0x40`, and one item is genuinely new:** across every
clip where continuing the walk past the declared track count reaches the declared end,
**exactly one block count lands — never two.** Ambiguity count **1.00**. That makes the
continuation a *location* rather than a coincidental fit, and materially strengthens the
"more payload, parses as more tracks" reading without saying what the extra blocks are. **⚠ REFUTED 2026-09-12 — see the control below; this claim no longer stands.**

> **⚠ THE AMBIGUITY ARGUMENT IS REFUTED (2026-09-12).** Team B ran the shift control and the result kills it. Their search tried **512 candidate block counts**; run from the real start versus a deliberately wrong start (+7 bytes, the displacement our own walk control uses):
>
> | start | clips finding a working count | mean working counts |
> |---|---|---|
> | **real** | **328 of 518 (63.32%)** | 1.00 |
> | **wrong (+7)** | **333 of 518 (64.29%)** | 1.00 |
>
> **The wrong start does marginally BETTER, with identical ambiguity.** So "exactly one block count lands, never two" was never evidence of a real location — it was a property of searching a space wide enough that a unique fit is guaranteed somewhere. **"328 reach `+0x30` by continuing" is therefore unsupported as evidence that extra track blocks exist at all**, and survives only as "a count was found", which is now a known-weak predicate.
>
> **This hits our own number too: `334 / 397` is the same measurement by the same method and inherits the identical problem. Do not build on it until it is re-run with the same shift control.**
>
> **⚠→✅ SUPERSEDED 2026-09-12: this refutation is itself refuted.** Team B's own control shifted the wrong anchor (the payload start, which corrupts the declared-track walk itself, instead of `end_of_declared_walk`) — that is why their wrong-start rate looked indistinguishable from their real-start rate. **Full resolution, with the mechanism and three converging lines of evidence, is in §27.2 item 1** — read it before citing anything in this box as current.
>
> **⚠ OUTCOME OF THAT RE-RUN (2026-09-12) — the refutation did NOT reproduce on our denominator, and the two results are now believed to be about two different instruments.** Re-run on our matching **397-clip** population (bit-carrying clips that walk but do not land directly), with two independent displacements: **real start 334 of 397 (84.13%)**, **shifted +7: 37 of 397 (9.32%)**, **shifted −5: 54 of 397 (13.60%)**. That is a large, two-sided separation — not the near-identity Team B measured.
>
> **The “denominator dilution” reconciliation is REFUTED by a feasibility bound** (pure arithmetic on the two published figures, not a cross-team measurement): the 121 clips in their population but not ours are those that fail the walk outright. **Even granting that every one of those 121 spuriously hit under shift, and taking our own 37 for the remaining 397, the ceiling is 158 of 518 = 30.5% — against their observed 333 of 518 = 64.3%, which exceeds that ceiling by roughly 2×.** Dilution therefore cannot account for the gap.
>
> ~~**Leading reconciliation:** the two measurements use predicates of very different candidate-space width — Team B's search tries 512 candidate block counts while ours continues a single walk deterministically, a candidate space of one, so both results are correct about different things.~~
>
> **⚠ THAT WAS MY OWN ERROR, REFUTED WITHIN THE HOUR, AND THE CORRECTION MATTERS MORE THAN THE HYPOTHESIS DID.** `spec-anim-format.md` §9.1 states its design explicitly: **“Same design (512 candidate additional block counts, real start vs. shifted)”** — the same predicate and the same `+7` displacement as theirs. I proposed a predicate-width explanation **without reading the design statement that was already in our own document**, which is the analogy-over-evidence failure §5 warns about, committed while writing up a lesson about exactly that.
>
> **So the discrepancy is HARDER than it looked, and it is an instrument disagreement.** Same predicate, same displacement, and our 397 is a **strict subset** of their 518 (their extra 121 being the outright walk failures). Under `+7`, ours finds **37 of 397**. If both implementations agreed on the 397 shared clips, their total could be at most **37 + 121 = 158**; they report **333**. **333 > 158, so the two runs cannot both be measuring what they say they are — no population composition can reconcile them.** At least one of the two shift controls is doing something other than its description.
>
> ~~Next step is therefore not theorising, it is diffing the two control implementations— specifically: what exactly is shifted (which anchor), and what counts as a hit (does the walk have to *land on* `u32(+0x30)`, or merely *complete*?).~~
>
> **⚠ THE WEAK-PREDICATE GUESS IS DIRECTLY TESTED AND REFUTED (2026-09-12), by sign, not just left unconfirmed.** Team B ran both predicates on their own `+7` control: **strict 333/518 (64.29%)**, **weak (walk merely completes, any of 512 counts) 390/518 (75.29%)**. A weaker predicate accepts a strict superset of hits, so it can only produce an equal or **higher** rate under a shift control — never lower. **Our 334/397 shift result was 9.32%, below BOTH.** A weaker predicate cannot produce a *more* discriminating control, only a faster-saturating one, so “ours used the weak predicate” predicts the wrong sign and is refuted outright. **The better-supported remaining explanation is the other guess: a different anchor being shifted.** Also newly on the table and needing the same explanation: **our real-start rate (84.13%) is meaningfully higher than their real-start rate (63.32%) on what is supposed to be an identical predicate** — that gap almost certainly shares a root cause with the shift discrepancy, and diffing the anchor should explain both at once. **Next step, narrowed: confirm which anchor each implementation shifts** — the payload start, the declared endpoint, or the candidate-count origin. Status remains **UNKNOWN pending that diff**, but the space of explanations has shrunk to one.

> **⚠ DECISIVE NEW DATA (2026-09-12) — the same predicate, widened to Team B's exact 518-clip scope, and the divergence is now precisely located.** Same `search()`, same anchor (`end-of-declared-walk + shift`), same hit criterion (`p == off30`, exact landing on `u32(+0x30)`), run over the full 518 rather than our 397 subset:
>
> | anchor | ours (n=518) | theirs (n=518) |
> |---|---|---|
> | real start | **334 (64.48%)** | 328 (63.32%) |
> | wrong +7 | **41 (7.92%)** | 333 (64.29%) |
> | wrong −5 | **56 (10.81%)** | — |
>
> **The two implementations agree closely on the real-start count** (334 vs 328, the same ~6-clip gap already known from the 397-clip scale) **and diverge catastrophically on the shifted count.** Ours drops **8×** under a `+7` shift (64.48% → 7.92%) — the signature of a search sharply sensitive to the true anchor, which is exactly what real byte-precise structure should produce. **Theirs is essentially FLAT under the same shift** (63.32% → 64.29%, an *increase*) — the signature of a search with no discriminating power at all, which is precisely the property that made their original refutation look convincing.
>
> **So the balance of evidence has reversed direction, though it is not yet settled: this looks less like our control being wrong and more like theirs lacking the sensitivity a control needs to mean anything.** Leading hypothesis, `HIGH CONFIDENCE — inferred, pending the diff`: their per-block walker validates less strictly than ours — a walker that advances by a size read from the bytes without checking the block's contents for plausibility will rarely desync even on garbage-aligned input, and a 512-candidate search over a walker that almost never fails will find *something* landing on the target from nearly any start, by pure combinatorics. Ours enforces the already-known field structure of a real block, so misaligned bytes desync it almost immediately. **This does not yet rehabilitate the original continuation-search claim** — our walker being more *selective* than theirs is not the same as proving that selectivity tracks the real format rather than some other strictness — but it does mean **the instrument that produced the refutation is the one now under the most suspicion, not the claim it refuted.**
>
> **Next step, sharpened to one specific diagnostic:** check whether Team B's per-block walk validates block contents or merely advances by a computed size with no validation — and separately, what fraction of their 512-candidate attempts per clip actually raise or desync versus complete cleanly, at both the real and shifted start. If their desync rate under shift is near zero where ours is high, that single number confirms the mechanism without needing a full line-by-line diff first.

> **✅ FULLY RESOLVED 2026-09-12 (`spec-anim-format.md` §10) — read this box and stop; every box above it in this item is now historical.** The layer-override-array tracing dispatched under the “never idle” policy found the actual mechanism, and it dissolves the entire thread above rather than settling either side of it. **There is a small table — a per-track count table, a value table, and a tail table — inserted between the header's `payload_start()` and where the ordinary per-track keyframe walk actually begins, present if and only if flags bit `0x40` is set.** Every walk-based measurement all day (the continuation search, the ambiguity claim, the anchor-bug saga, the 121/69-vs-135/55 bound) was starting one small table too early for this entire population, and everything each side observed was a byte-precise, reproducible artefact of that — not noise, not instrument disagreement, just the wrong start cursor for 518 clips.
>
> **Two independent disassembly sites now confirm the mechanism directly**, not by inference from correlation: `FUN_004c21a0` tests bit `0x40` to decide whether to *consult* the override arrays (found earlier today); `FUN_004c1190` tests the identical bit (`TEST AL,0x40` / `JZ`, raw instruction at `0x004c11a0`) to decide whether to *build* them in the first place, from the clip's own bytes, at exactly the cursor the ordinary walker starts from. **`0x40` is a genuine runtime layering flag, confirmed at the mechanism level, not a payload-location flag and never was.**
>
> **The empirical test, structurally different from every earlier walk-based claim in this thread — no candidate search, one deterministic formula:** skip the recovered table, then run the unmodified per-track walker once. **518 of 518 (100.00%)** land exactly on `u32(+0x30)`. Two shift controls (`+7`, `−5` bytes) collapse to **0.39%** and **0.00%**. Wrongly applying the same formula to the 3,691 bit-clear clips (which never build this table) collapses their own already-clean 99.89% baseline to **0.03%** — worse than chance, not merely unhelpful. All three controls behave exactly as a real, correctly-scoped structure predicts and a coincidence would not.
>
> **Still open, narrowly:** the multi-value override path (`FUN_004ca010`, taken when a track's override count exceeds 1) is untraced; a sibling function's distinct use of the same builder is a separate, unchased mechanism; the tail table's exact semantic role is recovered structurally but not interpreted. **None of this needs a dynamic pass** — the static evidence is decisive on its own terms.
>
> **✅ INDEPENDENTLY REPRODUCED, BYTE-FOR-BYTE, FROM PROSE ALONE (2026-09-12).** Team B wrote their own probe from this section's *written description* — never touched our harness, never touched our code — and it reproduced every published number exactly: 518/518 real, 3,687/3,691 sanity-unchanged, 0.39%/0.00% on both shift controls, 0.03% on the wrong-population control, 0/518 on both wide-width flags. In their own words, relayed verbatim: *“genuinely one of the best outcomes this project's had.”* This is a stronger form of confirmation than a second team re-running the same harness would have been — it validates not only the finding but the **prose describing it**: the spec was precise and complete enough that an independent implementation, built from words with no access to the reference code, landed on identical figures. Team B retired their own equivalent thread (their §9.38–§9.52) with pointer notices rather than deletions, consistent with the correction discipline both teams have held all day.

> **✅ RESOLVED (2026-09-12) — the anchor bug is found, and BOTH original findings are REINSTATED.** Team B found and fixed the actual defect in their own shift-control implementation: it applied the `+7` shift to the payload **START**, which also corrupts the declared-track-count walk itself — the part meant to stay fixed as a verified reference point — rather than to `end_of_declared_walk` (our anchor), which perturbs only the continuation search under test. **“Shift it by 7 bytes” never specified which anchor, and they had picked the wrong one without realising there was a choice.** Rebuilt with our exact anchor, on their own decoder and their own 518-clip population, three independent lines now converge:
>
> 1. **Count** — two independently-written walkers land within 4 of each other on the shifted count (37 vs 41; the residual gap tracks the same small ‘walk parses’ denominator difference — 383 vs 518 — already on record elsewhere, nothing new).
> 2. **Mechanism, not just correlation** — the desync-rate diagnostic named above: real start completes 5.92% of 512 candidates; their **original wrong anchor** also completes at 6.27%, indistinguishable from real (exactly why their control never separated); **our** anchor completes at only 2.04%, about a third of real — matching the 8× discrimination gap already found in the cross-run.
> 3. **Histogram** — under the correct anchor's shift, the extra-block-count histogram does not cluster at all (37 hits spread over 27 distinct values); their original wrong-anchor shift's tight pile at 42 (310 clips) was a symptom of *that walker's own corruption*, not evidence about the real data.
>
> **Consequence: both “ambiguity 1.00 — the continuation is a real location, not a coincidental fit” and “extra = 39 blocks in 310 clips” (the `auto_drv_*`/`auto_ridel_*` vehicle-occupant family at 46 tracks) are REINSTATED, `CONFIRMED — empirical`.** Team B's own retraction of both (their §9.44/§9.46) is itself retracted. **Every intervening boxed note above this one is superseded by this resolution and kept only as the record of how it was reached** — read them for the process, not for the current state.
>
> **Explicitly unaffected, and still the actual open question: what flag `0x40` does at *runtime*.** The disassembly finding stands untouched — nothing in the stream setup (`FUN_004c1020`, `FUN_004c1260`) tests the bit; only `FUN_004c21a0`, a layer-blend sampler, does, and there it gates a per-layer override lookup unrelated to payload location. **This resolved whether the continuation-search evidence for extra payload structure was real. It did not resolve what the bit means.** See §5 for the ambiguous-anchor-specification lesson this produced.
>
> **Decisive test, cheap on either side:** re-run **the narrow predicate** (single deterministic continuation, no count search) on Team B's population, and/or run **the 512-wide count search** on ours and check whether it saturates. If the narrow predicate separates on their data too, the continuation is rehabilitated for both teams. **Until then the status is DISPUTED, not settled either way** — and note what the rehabilitated version would license: *“there is real byte-precise structure at that location”*, **not** “the extra content is more per-track blocks”, which the disassembly separately does not support. Note the populations differ (our 397 bit-carrying walk-shorts versus their 518 bit-carrying clips), so **their control does not transfer arithmetically to our figure** — it has to be re-run on ours.
>
> **What survives, precisely.** The **121 outright walk failures** are determined by the shipping reader rather than by the search, and are unaffected. The **69-clip third population is arguably STRENGTHENED**: it is defined by *absence*, and a search that fires spuriously from any start about 64% of the time finding **nothing** for those 69 is a stronger negative than it appeared when the search looked trustworthy. The **turn-in-place signature is untouched**, because its decisive measurement — median root rotation exactly 90.00° from the header quaternion — never involved the walk.
>
> **And it converges with the `0x40` reframe rather than conflicting with it.** If nothing in the stream setup ever tests `0x40`, there may be no `0x40`-keyed extra-block structure to find — and *a search that succeeds from any starting offset is exactly what you observe when there is nothing real to lock onto.* Three payload models were refuted; the fourth possibility is that **the question was malformed**, which is a better outcome than a fourth refuted model.
Our landing rates do not disagree — my 334/397 excludes the ~121 that fail the walk
outright, their 328/518 includes them; same phenomenon, different denominators. The
~~extra block count remains **unpredicted**: 310/328 need exactly 39, 14 distinct values
overall, and `+0x0A`, `+0x0B` and bones-minus-tracks all fail (1/328, 1/328).~~

> **⚠ RETRACTED 2026-09-12 — “extra = 39” was search geometry, not a count.** Team B ran the same histogram against a shifted (+7) control and got **the identical tight clustering, centred on 42 instead of 39** — same 310 clips, same concentration, the number displaced by exactly the control offset. So the value 39 never described anything about the data. **What does survive, and the shift did *not* reproduce it: the same 310 clips group together under both readings**, so those clips genuinely share something real — identified as the `auto_drv_*` / `auto_ridel_*` vehicle-occupant family at 46 tracks. The grouping was the finding; the number was the instrument's shadow.
>
> **⚠→✅ SUPERSEDED 2026-09-12: this refutation is itself refuted.** The shifted histogram's own tight pile at 42 was the SAME wrong-anchor bug producing its own corrupted-input artefact, not evidence that 39 was search geometry. Under the correct anchor the shifted histogram shows no clustering at all, and the original 39-clip clustering is real. **Full resolution, with the mechanism and three converging lines of evidence, is in §27.2 item 1** — read it before citing anything in this box as current.

**And the sharpest remaining target is a third sub-population neither side had named:**
**190 clips never land at all**, even allowing 512 extra blocks — larger than the ~121
outright walk failures, so roughly **69 clips walk short AND are not explained by
continuation**. That is the next anim target when the zone work is done.


### 26.23 `.czn_pc` from an independent anchor — one controlled negative, two vacuous tests discarded

`spec-zone-data-format.md` §8. The idea was to stop walking the top level forward and
instead check it against a locator that does not depend on its lengths — §7.2's Mesh-block
finder. **One result survived; two were discarded as vacuous before publication.**

**Survived, and it is a negative:** a top-level record's body **never starts at the first
Mesh block** — **0 / 1,002**, against a control of 3 / 1,002 (0.3%), so the real reading
scores at or below chance. **The geometry is nested inside a record's interior, not
carried as a top-level section.** A reimplementation cannot reach geometry by walking the
top level. **[CONFIRMED — empirical, controlled.]**

**Discarded (a) — tautological.** "A declared chunk brackets the Mesh block in 830/1,002"
is guaranteed: chunks tile the walked span contiguously, so anything the walk reaches is
inside some chunk. **The arithmetic gave it away — the bracket count and the
"walk got past the mesh" count were the same number, 830.** Identical counts between a
test and its own precondition is the signature (cf. §26.16's byte-identical control).

**Discarded (b) — vacuous.** "Re-anchoring the walk past the geometry repairs 400 files"
raised clean termination from 260/1,002 to 623/987 and felt decisive. **600 of the 623
traversed ZERO records** — an immediate zero word read as a terminator — and controls at
bookend+8/+16/+64/+256 scored 52.4/51.6/36.7/54.3% against 63.1%. Zone files carry enough
zero padding that the test was nearly free.

**Two vacuous measurements in one pass, both caught by the same question** — what would a
failure have looked like — asked before publishing rather than after. That question has
now caught four things today (§26.14, §26.18, and both of these), which makes it less a
lesson than a required step. **This pass produced one negative and no positive finding,
and that is the honest total.**


### 26.24 `.cmorph_pc` dequantisation RESOLVED — the last parked item's core question

`spec-morph-format.md` §13. **The formula is recovered and empirically confirmed.**

**The chain:** customization assembler `FUN_009f36d0` → `FUN_009f9020`, which allocates a
pool literally named **`"temp_morph_mesh"`** and loops over `count × 0xC` →
**`FUN_009f6d30(target, srcPos, srcNrm, dst, float weight)`**. The pool name, the ×12 loop
stride and a float weight parameter identify it beyond doubt. Found by profiling a
**bounded** 144-function set taken from the assembler's own call list — the whole-binary
version of that same profile had returned 272 useless hits (§26.19).

```
pos[v].axis += ( i16(elem+0,2,4) / 32767.0 )             * w * A[axis]
nrm[v].axis += ( (u8(elem+8,9,10) / 255.0) * 2.0 - 1.0 ) * w * B[axis]
```

Constants read from the image as exactly `32767.0`, `255.0`, `2.0`, `1.0`.

**It corrects §3.5's element layout.** `+8` and `+10` were read as two 2-byte fields; they
are **three individual bytes** at `+8`–`+10`, one per normal axis, with `+11` padding. The
old note "`+10` as a u16 is always `< 4096`" was a **consequence** of `+11` being zero, not
a property of the format. **Predicted before measuring, then confirmed: `+11` is zero in
21,104,267 / 21,104,267 elements.**

**And it explains why `spec-morph-format.md` §12.1's identity search correctly found nothing.** `A` scales
**position**, `B` scales **normal** — two different quantities. Every candidate I tested
(`(min, extent)`, `(offset, scale)`, `(scale, center)`) presumed one quantity, so no
relation between the triples could have existed. **That negative was right and now has a
reason**, which is a better outcome than it being merely excused.

**Empirical:** decoded position deltas median **0.0037**, 99.85% within 0.25 units, max
3.36 — millimetres to centimetres for facial sliders, with body-scale targets in the tail.
Normal deltas max **2.00000**, identical to `max(B)`, which the formula forces. And a
range check kills the no-divisor alternative outright: `i16 × A` would give deltas to
**110,117 units**. **[CONFIRMED — disassembly + empirical.]**

**Still open:** the slider→weight remap is disassembly-only (no shipped file carries
slider values); whether normals are renormalised after accumulation was not traced. **→ Both were closed on 2026-09-12 (`spec-morph-format.md` §14, and §27.2 item 2): normals **are** renormalised, once per vertex at encode time; the remap is `lo`/`hi`/`hi2` with no curve. Left as written because this is a dated session entry — it records what was open *then*.**
**New harness:** `morph_decode.py`. **New scripts:** `FindMorphKernel.java`,
`WhoCallsKernel.java`, `ReadMorphDispatch.java`, `DumpMorphConsts.java`.
